Let me verify the file organization once more before doing the systematic audit. The 14b_scenarios.tex in the drafts folder is probably not part of the main paper, so I'll focus on the core files.

Now I'm checking the narrative consistency across the paper. The abstract frames the problem as estimating how many recursive generation iterations a text has undergone, and the introduction restates this same problem about whether recursive generation depth can be estimated from statistical properties. The contributions listed in the intro are the task formalization with the EDD metric and 180K benchmark, the Depth Degradation Atlas showing that perplexity tail features dominate with EDD=4, and systematic characterization across models and domains showing varying generalization rates. The methods section actually implements this through ordinal classification and benchmark construction with 180K samples across three depth levels.

The experiments validate the approach through in-distribution performance ranging from 68.9-85.9%, feature importance analysis, and cross-model and cross-domain generalization tests. The discussion explains why tail statistics matter more than means and identifies the full-replacement ceiling and scale dependence as key limitations. Now I'm verifying that the abstract's reported balanced accuracy of 59-82% and EDD reaching 4 aligns with what the experiments actually show.

The three main findings check out: depth signals concentrate in perplexity tail statistics (confirmed by classifier convergence and cross-perplexity features like Binoculars at 78.1-78.4%), decoding strategy impacts transfer more than model architecture (45.9% cross-strategy versus 55.4% cross-model), and the signal weakens at scale where EDD drops to zero at 14B despite maintaining 2× chance accuracy. These directly support the contributions stated in the introduction. The framing around empirical characterization through a pipeline of 19 features and random forest classification is consistent throughout, though I should verify there's no narrative drift where the introduction's emphasis on benchmark-driven studies aligns with the actual experimental approach.

I'm noticing a few cross-references to check: the introduction mentions validating contribution 2 through a 5-class extension (71-80%) which appears in the Atlas section, and contribution 3 references preliminary 14B results at 50.8% that get addressed in the generalization section. The abstract's claim about EDD reaching 4 across all three families at the primary 1-1.5B scale matches what the atlas confirms. I should also verify the baseline comparisons mentioned in the abstract—perturbation-based and membership-inference approaches—are properly documented.

Looking at the Discussion section, the strategy effect (9.3 percentage points) versus model effect (7.2 percentage points) aligns with the atlas results, and the full-replacement chain discriminability ceiling of 59-82% matches the main matrix. The 14B generalization point where EDD equals zero also checks out. One thing I'm noticing is that there doesn't appear to be a separate Conclusion section.

Now moving into the structural analysis of the paper. The Abstract covers the problem, method, and results well, though the significance statement could be more explicit about broader impact. The Introduction establishes the recursive loop problem and existing gaps, but I should verify how thoroughly it motivates why this matters.

Looking at the Related Work section, it's organized thematically rather than chronologically, which is good practice. The contributions align with the paper's structure—the task formalization maps to the Method section, the Atlas to its own section, and the characterization findings to the Generalization section. The title mentions "Estimating Recursive Generation Depth" which frames the core contribution clearly.

Now I'm checking whether the specific claims in the Abstract match the actual results. The Abstract claims 59-82% balanced accuracy across the model-strategy combinations, and I'm verifying this against the main results table to ensure the numbers are consistent. Continuing through the verification checklist—the perturbation baselines round to the expected range, Binoculars matches the table values, and the classifier models converge within acceptable margins. The abstract's claim about 19 perplexity-dominated features checks out, as does the intro's mention of effective dimensionality around 4-5 from multicollinearity, and the 9 configurations across three importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation, and Kendall's W) all align with what I'm seeing in the data.

The Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct instruction-tuned model shows that monotonic AUC decline pattern as stated, contrasting with its base model's non-monotonic behavior. The RF/XGBoost/LightGBM convergence within 0.4pp is confirmed in the generalization section. Now I'm checking the claim strength—the "confirmed by classifier convergence" language seems justified since the three models agreeing suggests the signal lives in the features rather than the classifiers themselves. The "structurally distinct degradation trajectories" phrasing is well-supported by the different AUC patterns across strategies, and the strategy effect magnitude appears to be around 9.3 percentage points based on what I'm seeing.

Looking at how carefully the paper hedges its language, the "implies" framing around decoding configuration is appropriately cautious. I don't see any SOTA claims, which is good. For "significant" usage, the paper includes a disclaimer about multiple comparisons not being corrected, and explicitly notes when results aren't significant—like the CORAL and DivEye comparison on Pythia nucleus. The curation proof-of-concept also states its results are non-significant. The paper's being quite measured about what it claims.

Now examining the experimental rigor, the main results table includes bootstrap error bars...

Checking the confidence intervals across different analyses—the cross-scale comparisons have 95% CIs, seed replication includes them, and the curation PoC reports standard deviations with p-values. The feature ablation and cross-model transfer sections lack explicit error bars in some places, though the latter uses cross-validation. For baselines, the paper compares against a solid range of recent methods spanning from DetectGPT through CORAL, plus zero-shot LLM approaches and end-to-end fine-tuned models.

The ablation studies are thorough—they test individual feature groups, vary the reference model, examine generation length effects, replicate across seeds, check temperature robustness, use mixed-depth corpora, analyze domain bias, and compare prompted versus BOS generation. On reproducibility, the appendix includes LoRA hyperparameters, GPU specifications (2× NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 with 48GB each), training time (~200 GPU hours), software versions across PyTorch and related libraries, and data preprocessing details.

Now checking the remaining reproducibility elements: Random Forest parameters are specified (200 trees, Gini criterion, no max depth), and there's a commitment to release code and data upon acceptance. The paper includes both a limitations section and ethics statement as required. One potential gap is whether the ARR/EMNLP 2026 checklist is included—it's typically a separate submission form rather than part of the paper itself, so I should verify this isn't an issue. Moving on to check notation and terminology consistency across the paper. Looking at the notation and abbreviation issues, I notice QWK and MAE appear in tables without prior definition, and while BOS and LOGO are standard or defined elsewhere, they could use clearer introduction. The section transitions flow logically from methods through theory to results and discussion, though the Discussion and Conclusion feels underdeveloped—it needs stronger connections back to the broader implications and a more cohesive summary of the overall narrative.

For the limitations section, I'm checking the coverage and word count. The paper addresses full-replacement versus accumulation as a substantive limitation, discusses scale boundaries and generalization constraints across model sizes and strategies, and mentions additional caveats like reference model dependency and adversarial robustness. The section runs around 140-150 words across the main paragraphs, which is borderline thin for a thorough limitations discussion.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/related_work.tex:6:Subsequent work refines the conditions under which collapse occurs: \citet{bertrand2024_stability} identify convergence to a fixed point under mixed real-synthetic retraining (providing theoretical grounding for our EDD ceiling, \S\ref{sec:method:theory}), \citet{shi2025_closer_look} reframe collapse as a generalization-to-memorization transition, and \citet{dohmatob2025_strong_collapse} extend guarantees to broader model classes.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/related_work.tex:7:[Omitted long matching line]
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/related_work.tex:33:Decoding strategies shape text statistics in ways that interact with depth signals \cite{holtzman2020_nucleus,finlayson2024_closing,garces_arias2025_decoding_decoded,wiher2022_decoding,basu2021_mirostat}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:46:Despite the 17.1~pp accuracy spread across cells, the perplexity tail statistics family (\texttt{p90\_ppl}, \texttt{p75\_ppl}, \texttt{kurtosis\_ppl}) and surprisal variance (\texttt{var\_surprisal}) dominate depth discrimination in every setting.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:48:Table~\ref{tab:feature_importance} shows the full per-cell ranking.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:102:We extend the benchmark to depth $d{=}5$ for all three models under nucleus sampling.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:108:The d3-vs-d4 AUC clusters near $T{=}0.75$ for all three models (Pythia $0.784$, OLMo $0.765$, GPT-2~XL $0.750$), while d4-vs-d5 converges to a narrow band ($0.681$--$0.684$, range $0.003$), suggesting an architecture-independent depth ceiling within the 1--1.5B nucleus setting.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:118:\caption{Binary pairwise AUC decay curves for three models (nucleus sampling, depths 0--5). Horizontal dashed lines mark $T \in \{0.65, 0.70, 0.75, 0.80\}$. All three curves converge below 0.70 at d4-vs-d5, while d3-vs-d4 AUC clusters in the range 0.750--0.784. Shaded bands: bootstrap 95\% CIs.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/conclusion.tex:10:The primary benchmark uses 1--1.5B parameter models with controlled LoRA fine-tuning.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/method.tex:12:This is an ordinal classification problem: depths carry a natural ordering, and $d{=}1$ is structurally closer to $d{=}0$ than $d{=}3$ is, since each generation step compounds distributional drift.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:41:Figure~\ref{fig:feature_trajectory} shows feature trajectories for nucleus ($p{=}0.95$) and temperature ($\tau{=}0.9$) sampling.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:42:Top-$k$ ($k{=}50$) trajectories follow the same monotonically decreasing trend for all features but with compressed inter-depth spacing, consistent with the signal attenuation effect discussed in \S\ref{sec:atlas:strategy}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:123:Both DeepSeek V3.2 and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct use identical prompting.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:125:The user message presents the passage (truncated to ${\approx}$384 words) and requests a JSON response containing an integer depth prediction (0--3), a confidence score, and a one-sentence reasoning.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:460:The base model exhibits non-monotonic decay with a U-shaped recovery at $d_{3\text{v}4}$, while the instruction-tuned variant shows strictly monotonic decay. Both variants exceed chance (AUC $> 0.5$) at all boundaries; depth signal exists regardless of instruction-tuning, though instruction-tuning strengthens and regularizes it.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:552:\paragraph{Outlier handling.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:555:Despite this variation, the classifier remains effective after clipping, confirming that the depth signal resides in the p90-and-below distributional shape rather than extreme outliers.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:563:$\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70} \geq 4$ was established by dedicated binary classifiers in the atlas (\S\ref{sec:atlas:edd}; d3v4 AUC: Pythia 0.784, OLMo 0.765, GPT-2~XL 0.750); the 5-class results provide complementary evidence that depth~4 remains discriminable in the multi-class setting.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:630:The narrow spread ($\leq$0.8\,pp across four methods) reinforces the conclusion that the 19-feature representation, not the classifier, determines the accuracy ceiling in the cross-model transfer setting.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:872:Under correct strategy routing, four-class accuracy reaches 72.6\% vs.\ 68.0\% under misrouting ($N{=}103{,}498$ / $76{,}502$), a gap of 4.6\,pp; d0 accuracy remains near-ceiling (${\sim}99.6$\%) regardless of routing, confirming that the d0 signal is strategy-invariant.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/introduction.tex:6:This model$\to$data$\to$model cycle is no longer hypothetical; it is already occurring in the wild. Yet whether the recursive generation depth of a text can be estimated from its statistical properties remains unstudied, a question that matters because first-generation synthetic text may be benign or even beneficial for training, while deeply recursive text drives the distributional collapse that degrades model quality \cite{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/drafts/14b_scenarios.tex:18:mirrors the 1--1.5B atlas ordering, confirming that strategy-dependent attenuation patterns generalize across scale.

dominate depth discrimination in every setting.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:48:Table~\\ref{tab:feature_importance} shows the full per-cell ranking.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:102:We extend the benchmark to depth $d{=}5$ for all three models under $0.784$, OLMo $0.765$, GPT-2~XL $0.750$), while d4-vs-d5 converges to a narrow band ($0.681$--$0.684$, range $0.003$), suggesting an architecture-independent depth ceiling within the 1--1.5B nucleus setting.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:118:\\caption{Binary pairwise AUC decay curves for three models (nucleus sampling, depths 0--5). Horizontal dashed lines mark $T \\in \\{0.65, 0.70, 0.75, 0.80\\}$. All three curves converge below 0.70 at d4-vs-d5, while d3-vs-d4 AUC clusters in the range 0.750--0.784. Shaded bands: bootstrap 95\\% CIs.}\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/conclusion.tex:10:The primary benchmark uses 1--1.5B parameter models with controlled LoRA fine-tuning.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/method.tex:12:This is an ordinal classification problem: depths carry a natural ordering, and $d{=}1$ is structurally closer to $d{=}0$ than $d{=}3$ is, since each generation step compounds distributional drift.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:41:Figure~\\ref{fig:feature_trajectory} shows feature trajectories for nucleus ($p{=}0.95$) and temperature ($\\tau{=}0.9$) sampling.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:42:Top-$k$ ($k{=}50$) trajectories follow the same monotonically decreasing trend for all features but with compressed inter-depth spacing, consistent with the signal attenuation effect discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:atlas:strategy}.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:123:Both DeepSeek V3.2 and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct use identical prompting.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:125:The user message presents the passage (truncated to ${\\approx}$384 words) and requests a JSON response containing an integer depth prediction (0--3), a confidence score, and a one-sentence reasoning.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:460:The base model exhibits non-monotonic decay with a U-shaped recovery at $d_{3\\text{v}4}$, while the instruction-tuned variant shows strictly monotonic decay. Both variants exceed chance (AUC $> 0.5$) at all boundaries; depth signal exists regardless of instruction-tuning, though instruction-tuning strengthens and regularizes it.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:552:\\paragraph{Outlier handling.}\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:555:Despite this variation, the classifier remains effective after clipping, confirming that the depth signal resides in the p90-and-below distributional shape rather than extreme outliers.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:563:$\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70} \\geq 4$ was established by dedicated binary classifiers in the atlas (\\S\\ref{sec:atlas:edd}; d3v4 AUC: Pythia 0.784, OLMo 0.765, GPT-2~XL 0.750); the 5-class results provide complementary evidence that depth~4 remains discriminable in the multi-class setting.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:630:The narrow spread ($\\leq$0.8\\,pp across four methods) reinforces the conclusion that the 19-feature representation, not the classifier, determines the accuracy ceiling in the cross-model transfer setting.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:872:Under correct strategy routing, four-class accuracy reaches 72.6\\% vs.\\ 68.0\\% under misrouting ($N{=}103{,}498$ / $76{,}502$), a gap of 4.6\\,pp; d0 accuracy remains near-ceiling (${\\sim}99.6$\\%) regardless of routing, confirming that the d0 signal is strategy-invariant.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/introduction.tex:6:This model$\\to$data$\\to$model cycle is no longer hypothetical; it is already occurring in the wild. Yet whether the recursive generation depth of a text can be estimated from its statistical properties remains unstudied, a question that matters because first-generation synthetic text may be benign or even beneficial for training, while deeply recursive text drives the distributional collapse that degrades model quality \\cite{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/drafts/14b_scenarios.tex:18:mirrors the 1--1.5B atlas ordering, confirming that strategy-dependent attenuation patterns generalize across scale.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/drafts/14b_scenarios.tex:20:%% diverges from the 1--1.5B atlas ordering, where temperature yields the highest accuracy; at 14B, [describe actual ranking], suggesting that scale interacts with strategy effects non-trivially.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/drafts/14b_scenarios.tex:48:% Preliminary cross-scale experiments (7--14B, 54--70\\%) provide an existence proof that depth signal persists at larger scales under nucleus and temperature sampling, while top-$k$ drops to chance, confirming that scale amplifies strategy-dependent attenuation.",

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:413:Both 7--8B models substantially exceed the 25\% chance baseline: Llama-3.1-8B achieves 70.2\% (95\% CI $[68.3, 72.4]$) and Qwen2.5-7B achieves 60.1\% ($[58.0, 62.1]$); recursive depth therefore remains estimable from perplexity-based features even when the generator is 5--7$\times$ larger than the reference model.

recursive depth therefore remains estimable from perplexity-based features even when the generator is 5--7$\\times$ larger than the reference model.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:554:The d3 perplexity distribution varies substantially across seeds: under seed=42, 0.56\\% of d3 passages exceed $10^6$ in raw perplexity, while under seed=43 this fraction rises to 12.8\\%.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:561:Five-class accuracy reaches 80.5\\% (OLMo), 74.0\\% (GPT-2~XL), and 71.3\\% (Pythia), all substantially above the 20\\% chance baseline.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:640:The top-5 features shift substantially: \\texttt{surprisal\\_range}, \\texttt{surprisal\\_position\\_corr}, \\texttt{prob\\_std}, \\texttt{surprisal\\_first\\_half\\_minus\\_second}, and \\texttt{surprisal\\_std} replace the perplexity tail statistics (\\texttt{p90\\_ppl}, \\texttt{var\\_surprisal}) that dominate in base models.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:810:At 14B, when only 1--1.5B reference models are available for cross-perplexity computation (Pythia-1.4B observer analyzing Qwen2.5-14B text), Binoculars features degrade substantially (Table~\\ref{tab:bino_14b_ablation}).",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01HXkthYJkR8uQVxaQohWp7s`</summary>

```text
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:37:Among generated depths, d2 consistently yields the lowest recall (44--73\%), followed by d3 (61--80\%) and d1 (72--91\%).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:38:The d2 trough reflects its position at the center of the depth gradient, where features overlap with both d1 and d3, consistent with d2-vs-d3 as the EDD bottleneck (\S\ref{sec:atlas:edd}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:47:Within this family, \texttt{p90\_ppl} ranks top-2 by Gini importance in all 9 cells and \texttt{var\_surprisal} in 8/9 under SHAP (7/9 Gini).\footnote{The group-level dominance of perplexity tail statistics is robust across importance measures (Gini, SHAP, permutation; Kendall's $W{=}0.90$, $p{<}0.005$).}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/atlas.tex:113:Top-$k$ configurations lose $\mathrm{EDD}{=}3$ first (Pythia top-$k$ at $T{=}0.70$, OLMo top-$k$ at $T{=}0.75$); d2-vs-d3 is the consistent bottleneck.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/conclusion.tex:13:Additional caveats---reference model dependency (${\leq}$11\,pp), single LoRA seed (${\sim}$1--3\,pp uncertainty), adversarial robustness, and passage length sensitivity---are detailed in the Appendix.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/method.tex:105:The RF produces ordinally consistent predictions: 98.2\% of errors fall on adjacent depths ($|\Delta| \leq 1$), QWK\,=\,0.89; CORAL ordinal regression matches within 0.6\,pp ($p > 0.05$; \S\ref{sec:generalization}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/theory.tex:10:Our empirical AUC-excess contraction (ratio 0.80 at d1v2$\to$d2v3, 0.69 at d3v4$\to$d4v5; \S\ref{sec:atlas:edd}) is consistent with geometric decay approaching a fixed point \cite{bertrand2024_stability}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:42:Top-$k$ ($k{=}50$) trajectories follow the same monotonically decreasing trend for all features but with compressed inter-depth spacing, consistent with the signal attenuation effect discussed in \S\ref{sec:atlas:strategy}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:53:Per-cell top-5 overlap between SHAP and permutation importance is 3--4 out of 5 features; the aggregated (mean-across-cells) top-5 sets are identical; the top features are therefore robust to the choice of importance measure at the aggregate level, though per-cell rankings exhibit model-dependent variation.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:141:\paragraph{Within-$\tau$ robustness.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:441:Among instruction-tuned models, Llama-3.1-8B exhibits monotonic AUC decay consistent with the 1--1.5B pattern, while Qwen2.5-7B shows a U-shaped recovery beyond d2v3 (Table~\ref{tab:cross_scale_auc}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:506:The observed 84.75\% with monotonically decreasing per-depth F1 (d0=0.98, d1=0.88, d2=0.76, d3=0.76) is more consistent with a depth-signal interpretation, as architecture fingerprinting would not produce this graded pattern.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:549:For Pythia ($+$0.14\,pp), CIs overlap, consistent with noise; for OLMo ($+$3.17\,pp) and GPT-2~XL ($+$2.04\,pp), CIs do not overlap, so LoRA initialization has a non-trivial effect on absolute accuracy (${\sim}$1--3\,pp).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:566:For Pythia and GPT-2~XL, the dominant depth markers (\texttt{p90\_ppl}, \texttt{var\_surprisal}) maintain monotonic decrease through d4 (Pythia \texttt{p90\_ppl}: $483.7 \to 12.5$ from d0 to d4; \texttt{var\_surprisal}: $6.50 \to 1.29$), consistent with continued tail erosion (\S\ref{sec:method:theory}).

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have enough information to compile the audit. Let me check one more thing: the number accuracy consistency between the Abstract and the tables. Let me also check the word count of the Limitations section more carefully, and check whether the contribution list items match the experimental sections.

Let me also check for a few more things:

1. The Abstract says "59--82%" for d1-d3 balanced accuracy. Table main_matrix shows d1-d3 values in parentheses: 71.8, 69.3, 58.9, 72.7, 76.6, 66.2, 79.6, 81.5, 68.1. 

The range is 58.9 to 81.5, so "59--82%" is accurate when rounding 58.9 up to 59 and 81.5 up to 82.

2. Abstract says "Perturbation-based and membership-inference baselines (29--33\%)". Table baselines shows DetectGPT 28.8%, Min-K% 33.2%. The exact range is 28.8-33.2%, which rounds to 29-33%, so this is acceptable.

3. Abstract says "Binoculars, 78.1\% vs.\ 78.4\%

": matches table.

4. Cross-strategy vs. cross-model numbers check out—the cross-strategy table shows 4-class accuracies with d1--d3 mean at 45.9%, and cross-model shows d1--d3 mean at 55.4%. ✅

5. At 14B, the accuracy is 50.8%, which is 2.03× chance level (25%), rounding to 2×. ✅

Now I need to look at the Discussion and Conclusion section structure—it's titled as one combined section but lacks a proper conclusion with contribution summary, future directions, or closing remarks. The paper's main.tex shows the sections flow through Atlas and Generalization experiments before reaching Discussion and Conclusion, which then leads into Limitations and Ethics Statement. The combined Discussion and Conclusion section feels underdeveloped for a 24-page paper.

Looking at the Discussion more closely, I see it does integrate experimental numbers meaningfully—like using the 25.1% perplexity result to explain why tail behavior matters, or comparing the 9.3 pp strategy effect against the 7.2 pp model effect as higher-level interpretation rather than mere repetition. The second paragraph similarly uses the d1–d3 range and accumulation numbers to build toward deployment implications. Even the third paragraph's reference to the 14B EDD result appears to be setting up a larger point.

The real structural gap is that there's no actual conclusion section—just discussion. The paper ends without summarizing what was accomplished or articulating forward-looking questions beyond scattered mentions like "remains open." The narrative itself stays consistent from abstract through methods to experiments, and the contributions align well with what's tested, but the ending feels incomplete. The language style is mostly clean—I've spotted a few em-dashes scattered throughout the abstract and appendix that could be tightened up, along with some informal word choices like "notable" and "leverage" that lean slightly conversational. Now I'm ready to finalize the report.
```

[CONTENT_AUDIT_START]

=== 叙事一致性（CRITICAL） ===

- [info] [叙事一致性] Abstract→Intro→Method→Atlas→Generalization→Discussion 全链叙事高度一致。Abstract 的"问题"（无法估计递归深度）= Intro 的"问题"= Method 的形式化定义。三条 contribution 分别对应 Method（任务形式化+EDD）、Atlas（Depth Degradation Atlas）、Generalization（系统性表征），无遗漏无溢出。定量 claim（59-82%, EDD=4, 45.9% vs 55.4%）均与实验表格吻合。

- [warning] [叙事一致性] discussion.tex: "Discussion and Conclusion" 标题声称含 Conclusion，但实际只有 3 段讨论，无贡献总结、无前瞻性展望、无核心 takeaway 重申。审稿人会认为论文缺少 proper conclusion。建议：要么拆分为独立 Discussion + Conclusion 两节，要么在末尾加一段 concluding paragraph 总结核心发现和开放问题。

=== 章节结构 ===

- [info] [章节结构] Abstract 覆盖四要素（问题/方法/结果/意义），~155 words，密度高。Introduction 遵循 背景→gap→方法→贡献 结构。Related Work 按 6 个主题分组，非堆砌式。结构整体规范。

=== 贡献-范围对齐 ===

- [info] [贡献-范围] 三条 contribution 每条均有对应正文 section/table 支撑，无孤儿章节。Title "via Statistical Fingerprints" 与 Method §3.3 的 19-feature 设计完全对应。Abstract "three model families, three decoding strategies, and four depths" 与 180K benchmark 匹配。

=== Claim 强度匹配 ===

- [info] [Claim] 论文对 claim 强度控制总体审慎：明确声明 "our contribution centers on task formalization and systematic empirical characterization rather than algorithmic novelty"（introduction.tex:19）；14B 结果标注为 "preliminary"；curation PoC 标注为 "inconclusive"。未使用 "state-of-the-art" 或 "groundbreaking"。

- [warning] [Claim] section_appendix.tex:351 "substantially outperforms" 用于描述 RF vs perplexity-threshold baseline（F1: 0.997 vs 0.845），无统计检验支撑。建议改为 "outperforms" 或附 p 值。

- [warning] [Claim] section_appendix.tex 多处使用 "substantially exceed"（如 :413, :561）描述超过 chance baseline 的结果，这些比较严格意义上不需要统计检验（因为 CI 不含 chance），但措辞可更精确为 "well above" 以避免暗示统计显著性。

=== 实验严谨性 ===

- [info] [实验] 主结果表有 bootstrap 95% CI（Table 1 main_matrix）。跨尺度实验有 95% CI。Seed replication 有 CI。方法学可靠。

- [info] [实验] Baselines 覆盖近 2 年文献：DetectGPT(2023), Binoculars(2024), Fast-DetectGPT(2024), DivEye(2026), Min-K%(2024), CORAL(2020), DeBERTa/RoBERTa end-to-end, zero-shot LLMs。Ablation 覆盖 feature group、reference model、generation length、cross-seed、temperature robustness、domain bias、mixed-depth corpus、prompted generation、cross-boundary chains 等 10+ 维度，极为全面。

- [warning] [实验] 主实验使用单一 LoRA seed (seed=42)。虽然 Appendix F.15 (seed replication) 报告了 seed=43 的结果（Δ最大 3.17pp），但主表只报告单 seed。论文在 Limitations 中承认此问题（"single LoRA seed (~1-3pp uncertainty)"），但审稿人可能仍质疑主结果的稳健性。

- [info] [实验] method.tex:104 脚注明确声明 "Statistical tests are not corrected for multiple comparisons; p-values should be interpreted as exploratory"，透明度好。

=== 可复现性披露 ===

- [info] [复现] 超参（LoRA rank/alpha/lr/batch/epochs）、GPU（2×RTX PRO 6000 48GB）、训练时间（~200 GPU·hours）、软件版本（PyTorch 2.10.0, Transformers 5.8.1 等）均已披露。Appendix A 有完整 LoRA protocol 表。

- [info] [复现] Limitations 和 Ethics Statement 均存在且有实质内容。

=== 符号/术语一致性 ===

- [warning] [术语] method.tex:105 首次出现 "QWK" (Quadratic Weighted Kappa) 未给出全称定义。同行 atlas.tex:21 也出现 "MAE" (Mean Absolute Error) 未定义。首次使用缩写必须展开。

- [warning] [术语] method.tex:33,35 "BOS" (Beginning of Sequence) 首次出现未展开定义。虽是 NLP 常见术语，但按规范首次使用应定义。

- [info] [术语] 核心符号（$\mathcal{D}_k$, $f_\theta$, EDD, $T$）全文定义一致，无同义词漂移。

=== 章节过渡与逻辑流 ===

- [warning] [逻辑流] Discussion and Conclusion 仅 3 段（~150 words），对于一篇 24 页论文显得过于单薄。相比之下 Limitations 有 2 段实质内容。Discussion 的 3 个段落（tails vs means / ceiling vs floor / scale dependence）各自独立，缺少统一的 concluding narrative 将它们串联。

=== Limitations 诚实度 ===

- [info] [Limitations] 包含 2 个核心段落和 1 个追加 caveat 列表。覆盖 full-replacement vs accumulation、scale boundary (14B EDD=0)、cross-strategy transfer (45.9%)、web-crawl FPR (22%)、reference model dependency、single seed、adversarial robustness。≥5 个 distinct limitations，实质性强，非回避式。

- [info] [Limitations] 字数约 140 words（不含 Ethics Statement），接近 150 word 下限但内容密度高。追加 caveats 指向 Appendix 展开。

=== 去 AI 风格 ===

- [warning] [去AI] abstract.tex:4 "pipeline---19 statistical features dominated by perplexity tail statistics, classified by Random Forest---achieves" — 解释性 em-dash，典型 AI 写作标志。建议改为括号或拆分句子。

- [warning] [去AI] abstract.tex:5 "signal attenuates with scale---at 14B" — 解释性 em-dash。建议改为分号或句号。

- [warning] [去AI] conclusion.tex:13 "Additional caveats---reference model dependency...adversarial robustness, and passage length sensitivity---are detailed in the Appendix." — 解释性 em-dash。建议改为括号。

- [warning] [去AI] section_appendix.tex:742 "78.1\%---matching our 19-feature pipeline" — 解释性 em-dash。

- [warning] [去AI] section_appendix.tex:812 "scale gap---though the gain is attenuated" — 解释性 em-dash。

- [info] [去AI] section_appendix.tex:570 使用了 "leverage"（"multi-class classifiers can leverage global distributional structure"），建议改为 "exploit" 或 "use"。

- [info] [去AI] section_appendix.tex:742 使用了 "notable exception"，建议改为 "exception"。

- [info] [去AI] 未检出 Furthermore/Moreover/Additionally、"it is worth noting"、"delve into"、过度分段等其他 AI 风格问题。正文段落长度合理。

[CONTENT_AUDIT_END]
```

**审计总结：**

这篇论文的内容质量很高。叙事一致性是最强项——从 Abstract 到 Experiments 全链条无漂移，contribution 列表与实验完全对齐，claim 强度与 evidence 匹配审慎。实验设计严谨，ablation 覆盖全面。

主要改进点：
1. **Discussion and Conclusion** 过于单薄（3 段），需要补充 proper conclusion paragraph
2. **5 处 em-dash** 需清除（尤其 Abstract 的 2 处最醒目）
3. **QWK / MAE / BOS** 缩写首次出现未定义
4. Appendix 中 "substantially" 用词可精确化

无 critical 级叙事问题。

[AUDIT_DONE]
